[BBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_News "BBC News") reported that Khem Lata Wukhloo, who was a leader of the Congress party at the time, admitted the widespread rigging in Kashmir. He stated: I remember that there was a massive rigging in 1987 elections. The losing candidates were declared winners. It shook the ordinary people's faith in the elections and the democratic process.[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-271)

Meanwhile, in Pakistan administered Gilgit Baltistan, the state sponsored [1988 Gilgit Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Gilgit_Massacre "1988 Gilgit Massacre") led by [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden "Osama bin Laden") and other extremist groups take place.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Undaunted-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-Undaunted)

### 1989 popular insurgency and militancy

Main article: [Insurgency in Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurgency_in_Jammu_and_Kashmir "Insurgency in Jammu and Kashmir")

> In the years since 1990, the Kashmiri Muslims and the Indian government have conspired to abolish the complexities of Kashmiri civilization. The world it inhabited has vanished: the state government and the political class, the rule of law, almost all the Hindu inhabitants of the valley, alcohol, cinemas, cricket matches, picnics by moonlight in the saffron fields, schools, universities, an independent press, tourists and banks. In this reduction of civilian reality, the sights of Kashmir are redefined: not the lakes and Mogul gardens, or the storied triumphs of Kashmiri agriculture, handicrafts and cookery, but two entities that confront each other without intermediary: the mosque and the army camp.

—British journalist [James Buchan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Buchan "James Buchan")[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEGuha,_India_after_Gandhi2008654-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEGuha,_India_after_Gandhi2008654)

In 1989, a widespread popular and armed insurgency[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-274)[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-275) started in Kashmir. After the 1987 state legislative assembly election, some of the results were disputed. This resulted in the formation of militant wings and marked the beginning of the [Mujahadeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mujahadeen "Mujahadeen") insurgency, which continues to this day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:16-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-:16)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-:8) India contends that the insurgency was largely started by Afghan mujahadeen who entered the Kashmir valley following the end of the [Soviet–Afghan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Afghan_War "Soviet–Afghan War").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-1)[\[261\]](#refnote-:8) [Yasin Malik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasin_Malik "Yasin Malik"), a leader of one faction of the [Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_Kashmir_Liberation_Front_(Yasin_Malik) "Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front (Yasin Malik)") (JKLF), was one of the Kashmiris to organise militancy in Kashmir, along with [Ashfaq Majeed Wani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashfaq_Majeed_Wani "Ashfaq Majeed Wani"), Javaid Ahmad Mir, and Abdul Hamid Sheikh. Since 1995, Malik has renounced the use of violence and calls for strictly peaceful methods to resolve the dispute. Malik developed differences with one of the senior leaders, Farooq Siddiqui (alias Farooq Papa), for shunning demands for an independent Kashmir and trying to cut a deal with the Indian Prime Minister. This resulted in a split in which [Bitta Karate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farooq_Ahmed_Dar "Farooq Ahmed Dar"), Salim Nanhaji, and other senior comrades joined Farooq Papa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-278)[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-279) Pakistan claims these insurgents are Jammu and Kashmir citizens, and are rising up against the Indian army as part of an independence movement. [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International") has accused security forces in Indian-controlled Kashmir of exploiting an [Armed Forces (Special Powers) Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_(Special_Powers)_Act "Armed Forces (Special Powers) Act") that enables them to "hold prisoners without trial". The group argues that the law, which allows security forces to detain individuals for up to two years without presenting charges violates prisoners' human rights.[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-280)[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-BBC) In 2011, the state humans right commission said it had evidence that 2,156 bodies had been buried in 40 graves over the last 20 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC-1)[\[265\]](#refnote-BBC) The authorities deny such accusations. The security forces say the unidentified dead are militants who may have originally come from outside India. They also say that many of the missing people have crossed into Pakistani-administered Kashmir to engage in militancy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC-2)[\[265\]](#refnote-BBC) However, according to the state human rights commission, among the identified bodies 574 were those of "disappeared locals", and according to Amnesty International's annual human rights report (2012) it was sufficient for "belying the security forces' claim that they were militants".[&#8202;](#ref-back-AI-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-AI)

India claims these insurgents are [Islamic terrorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_terrorism "Islamic terrorism") groups from Pakistani-administered Kashmir and Afghanistan, fighting to make Jammu and Kashmir a part of Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC-3)[\[265\]](#refnote-BBC)[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-283) Indian analysts[&#8202;](#ref-back-:17-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-:17) and the JKLF have accused Pakistan of training and backing terrorists.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-285)[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-286) India states that the terrorists have killed many citizens in Kashmir and committed human rights violations whilst denying that their own armed forces are responsible for human rights abuses. On a visit to Pakistan in 2006, former Chief Minister of Kashmir [Omar Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Abdullah "Omar Abdullah") remarked that foreign militants were engaged in reckless killings and mayhem in the name of religion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-287) Indian authorities said in 2008 and 2021 that militancy was on the decline.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:16-1)[\[260\]](#refnote-:16)[&#8202;](#ref-back-online.wsj.com-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-online.wsj.com)

The Pakistani government denies it supports terrorists, only saying it has supported "freedom fighters" in the past.[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-289) In October 2008, President [Asif Ali Zardari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asif_Ali_Zardari "Asif Ali Zardari") of Pakistan called the Kashmir separatists "terrorists" in an interview with _[The Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal")_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Dawn:_Zardari-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-Dawn:_Zardari) These comments sparked outrage amongst many Kashmiris, some of whom defied a curfew imposed by the Indian army to burn him in effigy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-291)

In 2008, pro-separatist leader [Mirwaiz Umar Farooq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirwaiz_Umar_Farooq "Mirwaiz Umar Farooq") told the _[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post "Washington Post")_ that there has been a "purely indigenous, purely Kashmiri"[&#8202;](#ref-back-washingtonpost.com-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-washingtonpost.com) peaceful protest movement alongside the insurgency in Indian-administered Kashmir since 1989. The movement was created for the same reason as the insurgency and began after the disputed election of 1987. According to the United Nations, the Kashmiris have grievances with the Indian government, specifically the Indian military, which has committed human rights violations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-washingtonpost.com-1)[\[276\]](#refnote-washingtonpost.com)[&#8202;](#ref-back-online.wsj.com-1)[\[272\]](#refnote-online.wsj.com)[&#8202;](#ref-back-unhchr.ch-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-unhchr.ch)

In 1994, the NGO [International Commission of Jurists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Commission_of_Jurists "International Commission of Jurists") sent a fact finding mission to Kashmir. The ICJ mission concluded that the right of self-determination to which the peoples of Jammu and Kashmir became entitled as part of the process of partition had neither been exercised nor abandoned, and thus remained exercisable.[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-294) It further stated that as the people of Kashmir had a right of self-determination, it followed that their insurgency was legitimate. It, however, did not follow that Pakistan had a right to provide support for the militants.[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-295)

### 1989–1990 exodus of Kashmir Pandits

Main article: [Exodus of Kashmiri Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exodus_of_Kashmiri_Hindus "Exodus of Kashmiri Hindus")

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/40px-Edit-clear.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail) | This section **may be [too long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Section#Section_size_policies "Help:Section") or excessively detailed.** Please consider summarising the material._(August 2019)__([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))_ |

Due to rising insurgency and Islamic militancy in the Kashmir Valley, [Kashmiri Pandits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmiri_Pandit "Kashmiri Pandit") were forced to flee the valley.[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-296) They were targeted by militant groups such as the [Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_Kashmir_Liberation_Front "Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front"), [Lashkar-e-Taiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashkar-e-Taiba "Lashkar-e-Taiba"), and [Jaish-e-Mohammed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaish-e-Mohammed "Jaish-e-Mohammed"). On 4 January 1990, [Srinagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srinagar "Srinagar") based newspaper _Aftab_ released a message, threatening all Hindus to leave Kashmir immediately, sourcing it to the militant organization [Hizbul Mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hizbul_Mujahideen "Hizbul Mujahideen").[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-297)[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-298) In the preceding months, around 300 Hindu men and women, Kashmiri Pandits, had been slaughtered and women raped. Mosque released statement in loud speaker asked Hindus to leave Kashmir without their women.
On 19 January 1990, Kashmiri Pandits fled from Kashmiri due to atrocities such as killing and gang rape.[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-299)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Rediff-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-Rediff)

On 21 January 1990, two days after [Jagmohan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagmohan "Jagmohan") took over as [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_(India) "Governor (India)") of Jammu and Kashmir, the [Gawkadal massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gawkadal_massacre "Gawkadal massacre") took place in Srinagar when the Indian paramilitary troops of the [Central Reserve Police Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Reserve_Police_Force "Central Reserve Police Force") opened fire on a group of Kashmiri protesters in what has been described by some authors as "the worst massacre in Kashmiri history" (along with the [Bijbehara Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bijbehara_Massacre "Bijbehara Massacre") in 1993).[&#8202;](#ref-back-VS-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-VS) At least 50 people were killed,[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-302) with some reports of the deaths reaching as high as 280.[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-303) In the aftermath of the massacre, more demonstrations followed, and in January 1990, Indian paramilitary forces are believed to have killed around 300 protesters.[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-304) As a [Human Rights Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch "Human Rights Watch") stated in a report from May 1991, "In the weeks that followed \[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[the Gawakadal massacre\] as security forces fired on crowds of marchers and as militants intensified their attacks against the police and those suspected of aiding them, Kashmir's civil war began in earnest."[\[289\]](#refnote-305)

The mass exodus began on 1 March 1990, when hundreds of thousands of Kashmiri Pandits left the state; of the approximately 300,000[&#8202;](#ref-back-306-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-306)[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-307)[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-308) to 600,000[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-309)[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-310) Hindus living in the Kashmir Valley in 1990, only 2,000–3,000 lived there in 2016.[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-311)

### 2000s Al-Qaeda involvement

Main article: [Al-Qaeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda "Al-Qaeda")

See also: [Allegations of support system in Pakistan for Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_support_system_in_Pakistan_for_Osama_bin_Laden "Allegations of support system in Pakistan for Osama bin Laden")

In a 'Letter to American People' written by [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden "Osama bin Laden") in 2002, he stated that one of the reasons he was fighting America was because of its support for India on the Kashmir issue.[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-312) While on a trip to Delhi in 2002, US Secretary of Defense [Donald Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Rumsfeld "Donald Rumsfeld") suggested that [Al-Qaeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda "Al-Qaeda") was active in Kashmir, though he did not have any hard evidence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-313)[&#8202;](#ref-back-SMH-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-SMH) An investigation by a _[Christian Science Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Christian_Science_Monitor "The Christian Science Monitor")_ reporter in 2002 claimed to have unearthed evidence that Al-Qaeda and its affiliates were prospering in [Pakistani-administered Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistani-administered_Kashmir "Pakistani-administered Kashmir") with tacit approval of Pakistan's [Inter-Services Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Services_Intelligence "Inter-Services Intelligence") agency (ISI).[&#8202;](#ref-back-315-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-315) In 2002, a team comprising [Special Air Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Air_Service "Special Air Service") and [Delta Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_Force "Delta Force") personnel was sent into [Indian-administered Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian-administered_Kashmir "Indian-administered Kashmir") to hunt for Osama bin Laden after reports that he was being sheltered by the Kashmiri militant group [Harkat-ul-Mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harkat-ul-Mujahideen "Harkat-ul-Mujahideen").[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-316) US officials believed that Al-Qaeda was helping organise a campaign of terror in Kashmir to provoke conflict between India and Pakistan. Their strategy was to force Pakistan to move its troops to the border with India, thereby relieving pressure on Al-Qaeda elements hiding in northwestern Pakistan. US intelligence analysts say Al-Qaeda and [Taliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban "Taliban") operatives in Pakistani-administered Kashmir are helping terrorists trained in Afghanistan to infiltrate Indian-administered Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-317) [Fazlur Rehman Khalil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fazlur_Rehman_Khalil "Fazlur Rehman Khalil"), the leader of the Harkat-ul-Mujahideen, signed al-Qaeda's 1998 declaration of holy war, which called on Muslims to attack all Americans and their allies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CFRKashmir-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-CFRKashmir) In 2006 Al-Qaeda claim they have established a wing in Kashmir, which worried the Indian government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-319) Indian Army Lieutenant General H.S. Panag, GOC-in-C Northern Command, told reporters that the army has ruled out the presence of Al-Qaeda in Indian-administered Jammu and Kashmir. He said that there no evidence to verify media reports of an Al-Qaeda presence in the state. He ruled out Al-Qaeda ties with the militant groups in Kashmir including [Lashkar-e-Taiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashkar-e-Taiba "Lashkar-e-Taiba") and [Jaish-e-Mohammed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaish-e-Mohammed "Jaish-e-Mohammed"). However, he stated that they had information about Al Qaeda's strong ties with Lashkar-e-Taiba and Jaish-e-Mohammed operations in Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-320-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-320) While on a visit to Pakistan in January 2010, US Defense secretary [Robert Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Gates "Robert Gates") stated that Al-Qaeda was seeking to destabilise the region and planning to provoke a nuclear war between India and Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-321)

In June 2011, a US [Drone strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drone_attacks_in_Pakistan "Drone attacks in Pakistan") killed [Ilyas Kashmiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilyas_Kashmiri_(militant) "Ilyas Kashmiri (militant)"), chief of [Harkat-ul-Jihad al-Islami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harkat-ul-Jihad_al-Islami "Harkat-ul-Jihad al-Islami"), a Kashmiri militant group associated with Al-Qaeda.[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-322)[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-323) Kashmiri was described by [Bruce Riedel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Riedel "Bruce Riedel") as a 'prominent' Al-Qaeda member,[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-324) while others described him as the head of military operations for Al-Qaeda.[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-325) [Waziristan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waziristan "Waziristan") had by then become the new battlefield for Kashmiri militants fighting [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO "NATO") in support of Al-Qaeda.[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_News:Ilyas-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-The_News:Ilyas) Ilyas Kashmiri was charged by the US in a plot against _[Jyllands-Posten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten "Jyllands-Posten")_, the Danish newspaper at the center of the [Jyllands-Posten Muhammad cartoons controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten_Muhammad_cartoons_controversy "Jyllands-Posten Muhammad cartoons controversy").[&#8202;](#ref-back-327-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-327) In April 2012, [Farman Ali Shinwari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farman_Ali_Shinwari "Farman Ali Shinwari") a former member of Kashmiri separatist groups [Harkat-ul-Mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harkat-ul-Mujahideen "Harkat-ul-Mujahideen") and [Harkat-ul-Jihad al-Islami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harkat-ul-Jihad_al-Islami "Harkat-ul-Jihad al-Islami"), was appointed chief of al-Qaeda in Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-rediff-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-rediff)

### 2008–present

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/60px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail) | This section needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **Needs more recent information, "recent" as in last 10 years**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information._(April 2025)_ |

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Kashmir_Solidarity_Day.JPG/250px-Kashmir_Solidarity_Day.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kashmir_Solidarity_Day.JPG) [Kashmir Solidarity Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir_Solidarity_Day "Kashmir Solidarity Day") on every 5 February is observed in Pakistan. This banner was hung in [Islamabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamabad "Islamabad").

In March 2008, two separate incidents were reported in Indian-administered Kashmir- a blast near the civil secretariat and high court, and a gun battle between security forces and militants which left five dead. The gunfight began when security forces raided a house on the outskirts of the capital city of [Srinagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srinagar "Srinagar") housing militants.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:12-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-:12) The [Indian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Army "Indian Army") has been carrying out cordon-and-search operations against militants in Indian-administered Kashmir since the violence broke out in 1989.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:12-1)[\[313\]](#refnote-:12)

Massive demonstrations followed a May 2008 decision of the [state government of Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Jammu_and_Kashmir "Government of Jammu and Kashmir") to [transfer 100 acres (0.40km2) of land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarnath_land_transfer_controversy "Amarnath land transfer controversy") to a trust which runs the Hindu [Amarnath shrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarnath_temple "Amarnath temple") in the Muslim-majority Kashmir valley.[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-330) This land was to be used to build a shelter to house Hindu pilgrims temporarily during their annual pilgrimage to the [Amarnath temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarnath_temple "Amarnath temple").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-:13) Indian security forces including the army responded quickly to keep order. More than 40 unarmed protesters were killed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-332-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-332)[&#8202;](#ref-back-333-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-333) The largest protests saw more than a half million people waving Pakistani flags and crying for freedom at a rally on 18 August, according to _[Time magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_magazine "Time magazine")_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-1)[\[315\]](#refnote-:13)[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-334) The situation drew international reactions from separatist leaders and the United Nations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Dawn:Mirwaiz-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-Dawn:Mirwaiz)[&#8202;](#ref-back-unhchr.ch-1)[\[277\]](#refnote-unhchr.ch) Following the unrest in 2008, secessionist movements got a boost.[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-336)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-2)[\[315\]](#refnote-:13) Such demonstrations have been aloof of the fact that the India government very regularly undertakes activities to uplift the Muslim community and donates lands and other properties to the systemized [Waqf Boards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Waqf_Council "Central Waqf Council").[&#8202;](#ref-back-337-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-337)[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-338) Despite the protests, [state elections in November–December 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Jammu_and_Kashmir_Legislative_Assembly_election "2008 Jammu and Kashmir Legislative Assembly election") in Indian administered Kashmir saw a high voter turnout of more than 60% of the total registered electors.[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-339)[&#8202;](#ref-back-340-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-340)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Election_Boycott_Graffito_-_Old_City_-_Srinagar_-_Jammu_%26_Kashmir_-_India_%2826552145580%29.jpg/250px-Election_Boycott_Graffito_-_Old_City_-_Srinagar_-_Jammu_%26_Kashmir_-_India_%2826552145580%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Election_Boycott_Graffito_-_Old_City_-_Srinagar_-_Jammu_&_Kashmir_-_India_(26552145580).jpg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/A_large_crowd_assembled_to_cast_their_vote_for_the_first_phase_of_General_Elections-2009_at_a_booth%2C_at_Shiv_Nagar_in_R_S_Pura%2C_Jammu_on_April_16%2C_2009.jpg/250px-A_large_crowd_assembled_to_cast_their_vote_for_the_first_phase_of_General_Elections-2009_at_a_booth%2C_at_Shiv_Nagar_in_R_S_Pura%2C_Jammu_on_April_16%2C_2009.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A_large_crowd_assembled_to_cast_their_vote_for_the_first_phase_of_General_Elections-2009_at_a_booth,_at_Shiv_Nagar_in_R_S_Pura,_Jammu_on_April_16,_2009.jpg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/A_large_number_of_voters_in_a_queue_to_cast_their_vote_at_a_polling_booth_near_Thiksey_Monastery_in_Ladakh_during_the_5th_and_final_phase_of_General_Election-2009%2C_in_J%26K_on_May_13%2C_2009.jpg/250px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A_large_number_of_voters_in_a_queue_to_cast_their_vote_at_a_polling_booth_near_Thiksey_Monastery_in_Ladakh_during_the_5th_and_final_phase_of_General_Election-2009,_in_J&K_on_May_13,_2009.jpg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/The_polling_officer_administering_indelible_ink_to_the_finger_of_a_woman_voter_at_a_polling_booth_of_Srinagar-_Badgam_Parliamentary_Constituency_during_General_Elections_2004_on_April_26%2C_2004.jpg/250px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_polling_officer_administering_indelible_ink_to_the_finger_of_a_woman_voter_at_a_polling_booth_of_Srinagar-_Badgam_Parliamentary_Constituency_during_General_Elections_2004_on_April_26,_2004.jpg)

Visual of voters during parliamentary general elections being conducted in Indian-Jammu and Kashmir in 2004 and 2009. A boycott graffiti seen in Old City, Srinagar.

In 2009, protests started over the alleged [rape and murder of two young women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Shopian_rape_and_murder_case "2009 Shopian rape and murder case") in [Shopian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shopian "Shopian") in South Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-341) The next summer again saw large-scale protests with the immediate trigger being a [fake encounter staged by the military in Machil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Machil_encounter "2010 Machil encounter"), [Kupwara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupwara_district "Kupwara district").[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-342) This [2010 Kashmir unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Kashmir_unrest "2010 Kashmir unrest") saw separatist sentiments, pro-independence slogans, protestors who defied curfews, attacked security forces with stones and burnt police vehicles and government buildings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-343)[&#8202;](#ref-back-nyt20100911-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-nyt20100911)[&#8202;](#ref-back-nyt20100804-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-nyt20100804) Security forces in Jammu and Kashmir fired live ammunition on the protesters, resulting in 112 deaths. The protests subsided after the Indian government announced a package of measures aimed at defusing the tensions in September 2010.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Reuters20100930-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-Reuters20100930)

Revelations made on 24 September 2013 by the former Indian army chief [General V. K. Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._K._Singh "V. K. Singh") claim that the state politicians of Jammu and Kashmir are funded by the army secret service to keep the general public calm and that this activity has been going on since Partition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-347)[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-348)[&#8202;](#ref-back-349-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-349)

In October 2014, [Indian and Pakistani troops traded LOC gunfire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%932015_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_border_skirmishes "2014–2015 India–Pakistan border skirmishes") – the small-arms and mortar exchanges – which Indian officials called the worst violation of a 2003 ceasefire – left soldiers and civilians dead. Thousands of people fled their homes on both sides after the violence erupted on 5 October.[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-350)

The [2014 Jammu and Kashmir Legislative Assembly election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Jammu_and_Kashmir_Legislative_Assembly_election "2014 Jammu and Kashmir Legislative Assembly election") was held from 25 November – 20 December. Despite repeated boycott calls by separatist [Hurriyat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Parties_Hurriyat_Conference "All Parties Hurriyat Conference") leaders,[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-351) elections recorded highest voters turnout in last 25 years, that is more than 65% which is more than usual voting percentage in other states of [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Deccan_Chronicle-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-Deccan_Chronicle)[&#8202;](#ref-back-in.com-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-in.com)[&#8202;](#ref-back-ndtv.com-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-ndtv.com) Phase wise voting percentage is as follows (table):

| Voting phases |  | Date | Seats | Turnout |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/J_%26_K_Assembly_election_2014.png/250px-J_%26_K_Assembly_election_2014.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:J_&_K_Assembly_election_2014.png) |  | 25 November | 15 | 71% |
|  | 2 December | 18 | 71% |  |
|  | 9 December | 16 | 59% |  |
|  | 14 December | 18 | 49% |  |
|  | 20 December | 20 | 76% |  |
|  | **Total** | **87** | **65%** |  |
| Sources:[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-355)[&#8202;](#ref-back-TO-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-TO)[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-357)[&#8202;](#ref-back-358-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-358)[&#8202;](#ref-back-359-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-359) |  |  |  |  |

J & K 2014 assembly elections voters turnout

The [European Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Parliament "European Parliament") welcomed the smooth conduct of the State Legislative Elections in the Jammu and Kashmir despite boycott calls.[&#8202;](#ref-back-prnewswire.com-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-prnewswire.com) The [EU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU "EU") in its message said, "The high voter turnout figure proves that democracy is firmly rooted in India. The EU would like to congratulate India and its democratic system for conduct of fair elections, unmarred by violence, in the state of Jammu and Kashmir".[&#8202;](#ref-back-prnewswire.com-1)[\[344\]](#refnote-prnewswire.com)[&#8202;](#ref-back-dailyexcelsior.com-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-dailyexcelsior.com)

On 8 July 2016, a militant leader [Burhan Wani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burhan_Muzaffar_Wani "Burhan Muzaffar Wani") was cornered by the security forces and killed. Following his death, [protests and demonstrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%932017_Kashmir_unrest "2016–2017 Kashmir unrest") led to an "amplified instability" in the Kashmir valley. Curfews were imposed in all 10 districts of Kashmir, over 100 civilians died and over 17,000 were injured in clashes with the police.[&#8202;](#ref-back-362-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-362)[&#8202;](#ref-back-363-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-363)[&#8202;](#ref-back-364-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-364) More than 600 have pellet injuries to the face. To prevent volatile rumours, cellphone and internet services were blocked, and newspapers were also restricted in many parts of the region.[&#8202;](#ref-back-365-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-365)

An attack by four militants on an Indian Army base on 18 September 2016, also known as the [2016 Uri attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Uri_attack "2016 Uri attack"), resulted in the death of 19 soldiers as well as the militants themselves.[&#8202;](#ref-back-366-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-366) Response took various forms, including the postponement of the [19th SAARC summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_SAARC_summit "19th SAARC summit"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-367-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-367) asking the Russian government to call off a joint military exercise with Pakistan,[&#8202;](#ref-back-368-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-368)[&#8202;](#ref-back-369-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-369) and the 'Indian Motion Picture Producers Association' decision to suspend work with Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-370-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-370) On the Pakistani side, military alertness was raised and some [Pakistan International Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_International_Airlines "Pakistan International Airlines") flights suspended. The Pakistani government denied any role and raised the issue of human rights violations by Indian security forces.[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-371)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Police_in_Kashmir_confronting_violent_protestors_December_2018.jpg/250px-Police_in_Kashmir_confronting_violent_protestors_December_2018.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Police_in_Kashmir_confronting_violent_protestors_December_2018.jpg) Indian police in Kashmir valley confronting violent protesters in December 2018

In the deadliest incident since 2016, [Jaish-e-Mohammed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaish-e-Mohammed "Jaish-e-Mohammed") (JEM) carried and claimed responsibility for [a suicide bomb attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Pulwama_attack "2019 Pulwama attack") on a military convoy in Pulwama that killed over 40 Indian soldiers on 14 February 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-372) In [retaliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Balakot_airstrike "2019 Balakot airstrike") 12 Indian fighter jets dropped bombs on a "terrorist camp" in Pakistan-controlled territory of Kashmir, allegedly killing around 350 members in terrorist camps. As India trespassed Pakistan's air space, the incidents escalated the tension between India and Pakistan starting the [2019 India–Pakistan border standoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_standoff "2019 India–Pakistan standoff") and skirmishes.[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-373)[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-374)[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-375)[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-376)[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-377) On 22 March 2019, a peace offer was fixed, ending the hostilities, and with both countries agreeing to fight terrorism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-378)[&#8202;](#ref-back-379-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-379)

In August 2019, India [revoked the special status of Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revocation_of_the_special_status_of_Jammu_and_Kashmir "Revocation of the special status of Jammu and Kashmir") through Parliament, [abolishing Article 370](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_370_of_the_Constitution_of_India "Article 370 of the Constitution of India") and rendering the state [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Jammu_and_Kashmir "Constitution of Jammu and Kashmir") infructuous. Further both houses of the Indian parliament passed a [bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_Reorganisation_Act_(2019) "Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act (2019)") to reorganise the state into two union territories.[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-380)[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-381) This was followed by a strict preventive [pre-emptive state lockdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932021_Jammu_and_Kashmir_lockdown "2019–2021 Jammu and Kashmir lockdown"), which lasted until 5 February 2021 (A preventative lockdown was put in place after the death of Syed Ali Shah Geelani for 2 to 5 days).[&#8202;](#ref-back-382-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-382)[&#8202;](#ref-back-383-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-383)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3522-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-:3522) LOC border [clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_border_skirmishes "2020–21 India–Pakistan border skirmishes") between November 2020 and February 2021 resulted in 24 deaths of both military personnel and civilians.[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-385)[&#8202;](#ref-back-386-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-386)

In April 2025, three suspected militants and an Indian army officer were killed in separate encounters in the Indian-controlled region. On 9 April, the [Indian army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Army "Indian Army") engaged militants in [Kishtwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishtwar "Kishtwar") district, resulting in the death of one militant, with two more killed in subsequent firefights. On 11 April, in the [Akhnoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhnoor "Akhnoor") area, Indian soldiers intercepted militants attempting to infiltrate from [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan "Pakistan"), leading to the death of an army officer.[&#8202;](#ref-back-387-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-387) On 22 April, Islamist militants [attacked a tourist spot in Pahalgam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Pahalgam_attack "2025 Pahalgam attack"), causing the Indian government to attack alleged terrorist camps in Pakistan, as part of a [four-day intense conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_conflict "2025 India–Pakistan conflict") the following May[&#8202;](#ref-back-us_dia_report-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-us_dia_report) that ended in ceasefire.[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC_IN_PK_live-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-BBC_IN_PK_live)

| - Statistics from the [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC "BBC") report "In Depth"[&#8202;](#ref-back-390-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-390)<br>- 525,000 refugees from Indian-administered Kashmir migrated to Pakistan and Azad Kashmir in 1947–48.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEKorbel,_Danger_in_Kashmir1966153-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEKorbel,_Danger_in_Kashmir1966153)<br>- 226,000 refugees from Pakistani-administered Kashmir migrated to India and Jammu and Kashmir in 1947 and 1948.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEKorbel,_Danger_in_Kashmir1966153-1)[\[375\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEKorbel,_Danger_in_Kashmir1966153)<br>- A minimum of 506,000 people in the Indian-administered Kashmir Valley are [internally displaced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internally_displaced "Internally displaced") due to militancy in Kashmir, about half of whom are [Hindu pandits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmiri_Pandit "Kashmiri Pandit")[&#8202;](#ref-back-392-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-392)<br>- Muslims form the majority in the [Poonch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poonch_district_(J&K) "Poonch district (J&K)"), [Rajouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajouri_district "Rajouri district"), [Kishtwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishtwar_District "Kishtwar District"), and [Doda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doda_District "Doda District") districts of the Jammu region. [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia "Shia") Muslims make up the majority in the [Kargil district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kargil_district "Kargil district") in the Ladakh region.<br>- India does not accept the [two-nation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Nation_Theory "Two Nation Theory") and considers that Kashmir, despite being a Muslim-majority region, is in many ways an "integral part" of [secular India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_India "Secularism in India").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hardgrave,_Robert._-India:_The_Dilemmas_of_Diversity-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-Hardgrave,_Robert._-India:_The_Dilemmas_of_Diversity) |  |  |  |  |  |  |

### Indian view

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Maharaja_of_Kashmir%2C_Hari_Singh_%281895_-_1961%29.jpg/250px-Maharaja_of_Kashmir%2C_Hari_Singh_%281895_-_1961%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Maharaja_of_Kashmir,_Hari_Singh_(1895_-_1961).jpg) [Maharaja Hari Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharaja_Hari_Singh "Maharaja Hari Singh") signed the [Instrument of Accession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instrument_of_Accession_(Jammu_and_Kashmir) "Instrument of Accession (Jammu and Kashmir)") in October 1947 under which he acceded the [State of Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir_and_Jammu_(princely_state) "Kashmir and Jammu (princely state)") to the Union of India.[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/India_disputed_areas_map.svg/250px-India_disputed_areas_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:India_disputed_areas_map.svg) Map showing [disputed territories of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disputed_territories_of_India "Disputed territories of India")

India has officially stated that it believes Kashmir to be an integral part of India, though the then Prime Minister of India, [Manmohan Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manmohan_Singh "Manmohan Singh"), stated after the [2010 Kashmir Unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Kashmir_Unrest "2010 Kashmir Unrest") that his government was willing to grant autonomy to the region within the purview of the Indian constitution if there was consensus among political parties on this issue.[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-394)[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-395) The Indian viewpoint is succinctly summarised by the Indian [Ministry of External affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_External_Affairs_(India) "Ministry of External Affairs (India)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-meaindweb-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-meaindweb)[&#8202;](#ref-back-meaindpdf-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-meaindpdf)—

- India holds that the [Instrument of Accession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instrument_of_Accession_(Jammu_and_Kashmir) "Instrument of Accession (Jammu and Kashmir)") of the State of Jammu and Kashmir to the [Union of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_India "Dominion of India"), signed by Maharaja Hari Singh (erstwhile ruler of the State) on 25 October 1947[&#8202;](#ref-back-398-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-398)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chief_Justice_JSAnand-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-Chief_Justice_JSAnand) and executed on 27 October 1947[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chief_Justice_JSAnand-1)[\[383\]](#refnote-Chief_Justice_JSAnand) between the ruler of Kashmir and the Governor General of India was a legal act and completely valid in terms of the Government of India Act (1935), Indian Independence Act (1947) as well as under international law and as such was total and irrevocable.[&#8202;](#ref-back-meaindpdf-1)[\[381\]](#refnote-meaindpdf)
- The [Constituent assembly of Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constituent_assembly_of_Jammu_and_Kashmir "Constituent assembly of Jammu and Kashmir") had unanimously ratified the Maharaja's Instrument of Accession to India and adopted a constitution for the state that called for a perpetual merger of Jammu and Kashmir with the Union of India. India claims that the constituent assembly was a representative one, and that its views were those of the Kashmiri people at the time.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sayyid_Mir_Qasim-2)[\[note 8\]](#refnote-Sayyid_Mir_Qasim)[&#8202;](#ref-back-400-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-400)
- [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1172](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1172 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 1172") tacitly accepts India's stand regarding all outstanding issues between India and Pakistan and urges the need to resolve the dispute through mutual dialogue without the need for a plebiscite in the framework of UN Charter.[&#8202;](#ref-back-401-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-401)[&#8202;](#ref-back-402-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-402)
- [United Nations Security Council Resolution 47](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_47 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 47") cannot be implemented since Pakistan failed to withdraw its forces from Kashmir, which was the first step in implementing the resolution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-telegraph2001-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-telegraph2001) India is also of the view that Resolution 47 is obsolete, since the geography and demographics of the region have permanently altered since it adoption.[&#8202;](#ref-back-404-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-404) The resolution was passed by [United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council "United Nations Security Council") under [Chapter VI of the United Nations Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapter_VI_of_the_United_Nations_Charter "Chapter VI of the United Nations Charter") and as such is non-binding with no mandatory enforceability, as opposed to resolutions passed under Chapter VII.[&#8202;](#ref-back-pakun.org-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-pakun.org)[&#8202;](#ref-back-dawn.com-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-dawn.com)
- India does not accept the [two-nation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Nation_Theory "Two Nation Theory") that forms the basis of Pakistan's claims and considers that Kashmir, despite being a Muslim-majority region, is in many ways an "integral part" of [secular India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_India "Secularism in India").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hardgrave,_Robert._-India:_The_Dilemmas_of_Diversity-1)[\[377\]](#refnote-Hardgrave,_Robert._-India:_The_Dilemmas_of_Diversity)
- The state of Jammu and Kashmir was provided with significant autonomy under [Article 370](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_370 "Article 370") of the [Constitution of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_India "Constitution of India").[&#8202;](#ref-back-407-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-407)
- All differences between India and Pakistan, including Kashmir, need to be settled through bilateral negotiations as agreed to by the two countries under the Simla Agreement signed on 2 July 1972.[&#8202;](#ref-back-408-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-408)

Additional Indian viewpoints regarding the broader debate over the Kashmir conflict include:

- In a diverse country like India, disaffection and discontent are not uncommon. Indian democracy has the necessary resilience to accommodate genuine grievances within the framework of India's sovereignty, unity, and integrity. The Government of India has expressed its willingness to accommodate the legitimate political demands of the people of the state of Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-meaindweb-1)[\[380\]](#refnote-meaindweb)
- Insurgency and terrorism in Kashmir is deliberately fuelled by Pakistan to create instability in the region.[&#8202;](#ref-back-409-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-409) The Government of India has repeatedly accused Pakistan of waging a [proxy war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxy_war "Proxy war") in Kashmir by providing weapons and financial assistance to [terrorist groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_and_state_terrorism "Pakistan and state terrorism") in the region.[&#8202;](#ref-back-410-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-410)[&#8202;](#ref-back-411-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-411)[&#8202;](#ref-back-412-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-412)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Dawn:_Congress-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-Dawn:_Congress)
- Pakistan is trying to raise anti-India sentiment among the people of Kashmir by spreading false propaganda against India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-propaganda-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-propaganda) According to the state government of Jammu and Kashmir, Pakistani radio and television channels deliberately spread "hate and venom" against India to alter Kashmiri opinion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-415-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-415)
- India has asked the United Nations not to leave unchallenged or unaddressed the claims of moral, political, and diplomatic support for terrorism, which were clearly in contravention of [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1373](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1373 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 1373"). This is a [Chapter VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapter_VII_of_the_United_Nations_Charter "Chapter VII of the United Nations Charter") resolution that makes it mandatory for member states to not provide active or passive support to terrorist organisations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-416-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-416)[&#8202;](#ref-back-417-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-417) Specifically, it has pointed out that the Pakistani government continues to support various terrorist organisations, such as [Jaish-e-Mohammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaish-e-Mohammed "Jaish-e-Mohammed") and [Lashkar-e-Taiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashkar-e-Taiba "Lashkar-e-Taiba"), in direct violation of this resolution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-418-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-418)
- India points out reports by human rights organisations condemning Pakistan for the lack of civic liberties in Pakistani-administered Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-propaganda-1)[\[398\]](#refnote-propaganda)[&#8202;](#ref-back-419-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-419) According to India, most regions of Pakistani Kashmir, especially Northern Areas, continue to suffer from lack of political recognition, economic development, and basic fundamental rights.[&#8202;](#ref-back-420-0)[\[404\]](#refnote-420)
- [Karan Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karan_Singh "Karan Singh"), the son of the last ruler of the princely state of Kashmir and Jammu, has said that the Instrument of Accession signed by his father was the same as signed by other states. He opined that Kashmir was therefore a part of India, and that its special status granted by [Article 370](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_370 "Article 370") of the Indian Constitution stemmed from the fact that it had its own constitution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-421-0)[\[405\]](#refnote-421)

According to a poll in an Indian newspaper Indians were keener to keep control of Kashmir than Pakistanis. 67% of urban Indians want New Delhi to be in full control of Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-reuters.com-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-reuters.com)

[Michigan State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_State_University "Michigan State University") scholar Baljit Singh, interviewing Indian foreign policy experts in 1965, found that 77 percent of them favoured discussions with Pakistan on all outstanding problems including the Kashmir dispute. However, only 17 percent were supportive of holding a plebiscite in Kashmir. The remaining 60 percent were pessimistic of a solution due to a distrust of Pakistan or a perception of threats to India's internal institutions. They contended that India's secularism was far from stable and the possibility of Kashmir separating from India or joining Pakistan would endanger Hindu–Muslim relations in India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-423-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-423)

In 2008, the death toll from the last 20 years was estimated by Indian authorities to be over 47,000.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hindustantimes.com-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-hindustantimes.com)

In 2017 India's Union Home Minister, [Rajnath Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajnath_Singh "Rajnath Singh"), demanded that Pakistan desist from demanding a plebiscite in Jammu and Kashmir, saying: "If at all a referendum is required, it is needed in Pakistan, where people should be asked whether they want to continue in Pakistan or are demanding the country's merger with India".[&#8202;](#ref-back-425-0)[\[409\]](#refnote-425)

### Pakistani view

Pakistan maintains that Kashmir is the "jugular vein of Pakistan"[&#8202;](#ref-back-dawndec2006-0)[\[410\]](#refnote-dawndec2006) and a currently disputed territory whose final status must be determined by the people of Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-427-0)[\[411\]](#refnote-427)[&#8202;](#ref-back-428-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-428) Pakistan's claims to the disputed region are based on the rejection of Indian claims to Kashmir, namely the Instrument of Accession. Pakistan insists that the Maharaja was not a popular leader, and was regarded as a tyrant by most Kashmiris. Pakistan maintains that the Maharaja used brute force to suppress the population.[&#8202;](#ref-back-429-0)[\[413\]](#refnote-429)

Pakistan claims that Indian forces were in Kashmir before the Instrument of Accession was signed with India, and that therefore Indian troops were in Kashmir in violation of the [Standstill Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standstill_Agreement "Standstill Agreement"), which was designed to maintain the status quo in Kashmir (although India was not signatory to the Agreement, which was signed between Pakistan and the Hindu ruler of Jammu and Kashmir).[&#8202;](#ref-back-430-0)[\[414\]](#refnote-430)[&#8202;](#ref-back-431-0)[\[415\]](#refnote-431)

From 1990 to 1999, some organisations reported that the [Indian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Armed_Forces "Indian Armed Forces"), its paramilitary groups, and counter-insurgent militias were responsible for the deaths of 4,501 Kashmiri civilians. During the same period, there were records of 4,242 women between the ages of 7–70 being raped.[&#8202;](#ref-back-432-0)[\[416\]](#refnote-432)[&#8202;](#ref-back-433-0)[\[417\]](#refnote-433) Similar allegations were also made by some human rights organisations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-434-0)[\[418\]](#refnote-434)

In short, Pakistan holds that:

- The popular Kashmiri insurgency demonstrates that the Kashmiri people no longer wish to remain within India. Pakistan suggests that this means that Kashmir either wants to be with Pakistan or independent.[&#8202;](#ref-back-435-0)[\[419\]](#refnote-435)
- According to the [two-nation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-nation_theory "Two-nation theory"), one of the principles that is cited for the partition that created India and Pakistan, Kashmir should have been with Pakistan, because it has a Muslim majority.
- India has shown disregard for the resolutions of the UN Security Council and the United Nations Commission in India and Pakistan by failing to hold a plebiscite to determine the future allegiance of the state.[&#8202;](#ref-back-436-0)[\[420\]](#refnote-436)
- The reason for India's disregard of the resolutions of the UN Security Council was given by India's Defense Minister, Kirshnan Menon, who said: "Kashmir would vote to join Pakistan and no Indian Government responsible for agreeing to plebiscite would survive."[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kashmir_Old_Headache-0)[\[421\]](#refnote-Kashmir_Old_Headache)
- Pakistan was of the view that the Maharaja of Kashmir had no right to call in the Indian Army, because it held that the Maharaja of Kashmir was not a hereditary ruler and was merely a British appointee, after the British defeated Ranjit Singh who ruled the area before the British conquest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-438-0)[\[422\]](#refnote-438)
- Pakistan has noted the widespread use of [extrajudicial killings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing "Extrajudicial killing") in Indian-administered Kashmir carried out by Indian security forces while claiming they were caught up in encounters with militants. These encounters are commonplace in Indian-administered Kashmir. The encounters go largely uninvestigated by the authorities, and the perpetrators are spared criminal prosecution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-439-0)[\[423\]](#refnote-439)[&#8202;](#ref-back-440-0)[\[424\]](#refnote-440)
- Pakistan disputes claims by India with reference to the Simla Agreement that UN resolutions on Kashmir have lost their relevance. It argues that legally and politically, UN Resolutions cannot be superseded without the UN Security Council adopting a resolution to that effect. It also maintains the Simla Agreement emphasised exploring a peaceful bilateral outcome, without excluding the role of UN and other negotiations. This is based on its interpretation of Article 1(i) stating "the principles and purposes of the Charter of the United Nations shall govern the relations between the two countries".[&#8202;](#ref-back-441-0)[\[425\]](#refnote-441)

Human rights organisations have strongly condemned Indian troops for widespread rape and murder of innocent civilians while accusing these civilians of being militants.[&#8202;](#ref-back-442-0)[\[426\]](#refnote-442)[&#8202;](#ref-back-443-0)[\[427\]](#refnote-443)[&#8202;](#ref-back-444-0)[\[428\]](#refnote-444)

- The Chenab formula was a compromise proposed in the 1960s, in which the Kashmir valley and other Muslim-dominated areas north of the [Chenab river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chenab_river "Chenab river") would go to Pakistan, and Jammu and other Hindu-dominated regions would go to India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-445-0)[\[429\]](#refnote-445)

A poll by an Indian newspaper shows 48% of Pakistanis want Islamabad "to take full control" of Kashmir, while 47% of Pakistanis support Kashmiri independence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-reuters.com-1)[\[406\]](#refnote-reuters.com)

Former Pakistani President General [Pervez Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf "Pervez Musharraf") on 16 October 2014 said that Pakistan needs to incite those fighting in Kashmir,[&#8202;](#ref-back-tribune.com.pk-0)[\[430\]](#refnote-tribune.com.pk)[&#8202;](#ref-back-timesofindia.indiatimes.com-0)[\[431\]](#refnote-timesofindia.indiatimes.com) "We have source (in Kashmir) besides the (Pakistan) army…People in Kashmir are fighting against (India). We just need to incite them," Musharraf told a TV channel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-tribune.com.pk-1)[\[430\]](#refnote-tribune.com.pk)[&#8202;](#ref-back-timesofindia.indiatimes.com-1)[\[431\]](#refnote-timesofindia.indiatimes.com)

In 2015 Pakistan's outgoing National Security Advisor [Sartaj Aziz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sartaj_Aziz "Sartaj Aziz") said that Pakistan wished to have third party mediation on Kashmir, but it was unlikely to happen unless by international pressure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pak_wishes_to_have_3rd_party_mediation_on_Kashmir:_Aziz-0)[\[432\]](#refnote-Pak_wishes_to_have_3rd_party_mediation_on_Kashmir:_Aziz) "Under [Shimla Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simla_Agreement "Simla Agreement") it was decided that India and Pakistan would resolve their disputes bilaterally," Aziz said. "Such bilateral talks have not yielded any results for the last 40 years. So then what is the solution?"[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pak_wishes_to_have_3rd_party_mediation_on_Kashmir:_Aziz-1)[\[432\]](#refnote-Pak_wishes_to_have_3rd_party_mediation_on_Kashmir:_Aziz)

### Chinese view

See also: [Origins of the Sino-Indian border dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origins_of_the_Sino-Indian_border_dispute "Origins of the Sino-Indian border dispute")

China has generally supported Pakistan against India on Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:17-1)[\[268\]](#refnote-:17)[&#8202;](#ref-back-449-0)[\[433\]](#refnote-449) China has also stated that [Aksai Chin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aksai_Chin "Aksai Chin") is an integral part of China and does not recognise its inclusion in the Kashmir region. It also disputes the region's boundary with [Tibet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibet_Autonomous_Region "Tibet Autonomous Region") at various locations.

- China did not accept the boundaries of the princely state of Kashmir and Jammu, north of Aksai Chin and the [Karakoram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karakoram "Karakoram") as proposed by the British.[&#8202;](#ref-back-britannica1-0)[\[434\]](#refnote-britannica1)
- China settled its border disputes with Pakistan under the 1963 [Sino-Pakistan Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Pakistan_Agreement "Sino-Pakistan Agreement") on the [Trans-Karakoram Tract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Karakoram_Tract "Trans Karakoram Tract") with the provision that the settlement was subject to the final solution of the Kashmir dispute.[&#8202;](#ref-back-451-0)[\[435\]](#refnote-451)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Time-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-Time)

### Kashmiri views

- Scholar Andrew Whitehead states that Kashmiris view Kashmir as having been ruled by their own in 1586. Since then, they believe, it has been ruled in succession by the [Mughals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire "Mughal Empire"), [Afghans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durrani_Empire "Durrani Empire"), [Sikhs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikh_Empire "Sikh Empire"), [Dogras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dogra_dynasty "Dogra dynasty") and, lately, the Indian government. Whitehead states that this is only partly true: the Mughals lavished much affection and resources on Kashmir, the Dogras made Srinagar their capital next only to their native Jammu city, and through much of the post-independence India, Kashmiri Muslims headed the state government. According to Whitehead, Kashmiris bear an 'acute sense of grievance' that they were not in control of their own fate for centuries.[&#8202;](#ref-back-452-0)[\[436\]](#refnote-452)
- [A. G. Noorani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._G._Noorani "A. G. Noorani"), a constitutional expert, says the people of Kashmir are 'very much' a party to the dispute.[&#8202;](#ref-back-453-0)[\[437\]](#refnote-453)
- According to an opinion poll conducted by [Centre for the Study of Developing Societies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_the_Study_of_Developing_Societies "Centre for the Study of Developing Societies") in 2007, 87% of people in mainly Muslim Srinagar want independence, whereas 95% of the people in the mainly Hindu Jammu city think the state should be part of India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-0)[\[438\]](#refnote-:5) The Kashmir Valley is the only region of the former princely state where the majority of the population is unhappy with its current status. The Hindus of Jammu and Buddhists of Ladakh are content under Indian administration. Muslims of Azad Kashmir and Northern Areas are content under Pakistani administration. Kashmir Valley's Muslims want to change their national status to independence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTESchaffer,_The_Limits_of_Influence20093-0)[\[439\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTESchaffer,_The_Limits_of_Influence20093)
- Scholar A.G. Noorani testifies that Kashmiris want a plebiscite to achieve freedom.[&#8202;](#ref-back-456-0)[\[440\]](#refnote-456) Zutshi states the people of Poonch and Gilgit may have had a chance to determine their future but the Kashmiri was lost in the process.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEZutshi,_Languages_of_Belonging2004309-0)[\[441\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEZutshi,_Languages_of_Belonging2004309)
- Since the 1947 accession of Kashmir to India was _provisional_ and _conditional_ on the wishes of the people,[&#8202;](#ref-back-tandfonline.com-0)[\[442\]](#refnote-tandfonline.com) the Kashmiris' right to determine their future was recognised.[&#8202;](#ref-back-459-0)[\[443\]](#refnote-459) Noorani notes that state elections do not satisfy this requirement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-460-0)[\[444\]](#refnote-460)
- Kashmiris assert that except for 1977 and 1983 elections, no state election has been fair.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEMridu_Rai,_Hindu_Rulers,_Muslim_Subjects2004289-1)[\[234\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEMridu_Rai,_Hindu_Rulers,_Muslim_Subjects2004289) According to scholar [Sumantra Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumantra_Bose "Sumantra Bose"), India was determined to stop fair elections since that would have meant that elections would be won by those unfriendly to India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Bose_p.84-3)[\[227\]](#refnote-Bose_p.84)
- The Kashmiri people have still not been able to exercise the right to self-determination and this was the conclusion of the International Commission of Jurists in 1994.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hassan_Abbas-0)[\[445\]](#refnote-Hassan_Abbas)
- Ayesha Parvez writes in _[The Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hindu "The Hindu")_ that high voter turnout in Kashmir cannot be interpreted as a sign of acceptance of Indian rule. Voters vote due to varying factors such as development, effective local governance and economy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-462-0)[\[446\]](#refnote-462)
- The Hurriyat parties do not want to participate in elections under the framework of the Indian Constitution. Elections held by India are seen as a diversion from the main issue of self-determination.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTESchofield,_Kashmir_in_Conflict2003168-0)[\[447\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTESchofield,_Kashmir_in_Conflict2003168)
- Kashmiri opponents to Indian rule maintain that India has stationed 600,000 Indian troops in what is the highest ratio of troops to civilian density in the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTESchofield,_Kashmir_in_Conflict2003168-1)[\[447\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTESchofield,_Kashmir_in_Conflict2003168)
- Kashmiri scholars say that India's military occupation inflicts violence and humiliation on Kashmiris. Indian forces are responsible for human rights abuses and terror against the local population and have killed tens of thousands of civilians. India's state forces have used rape as a cultural weapon of war against Kashmiris and rape has extraordinarily high incidence in Kashmir as compared to other conflict zones of the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-464-0)[\[448\]](#refnote-464) Militants are also guilty of crimes but their crimes cannot be compared with the scale of abuses by Indian forces for which justice is yet to be delivered.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Kashmiri scholars say that India's reneging on promise of plebiscite, violations of constitutional provisions of Kashmir's autonomy and subversion of the democratic process led to the rebellion of 1989–1990.[&#8202;](#ref-back-465-0)[\[449\]](#refnote-465)
- According to historian Mridu Rai, the majority of Kashmiri Muslims believe they are scarcely better off under Indian rule than the 101 years of Dogra rule.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEMridu_Rai,_Hindu_Rulers,_Muslim_Subjects2004288-0)[\[450\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEMridu_Rai,_Hindu_Rulers,_Muslim_Subjects2004288)
- [Markandey Katju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markandey_Katju "Markandey Katju"), an ethnic Kashmiri and former Justice of the [Supreme Court of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_India "Supreme Court of India"), maintains that the secession of Kashmir would cause its economy to suffer, due to the fact that Kashmir's handicraft industry is dependent on buyers in other parts of India; Katju holds that the ultimate solution to the Kashmir conflict is the [reunification of what is now Pakistan with India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reunification "Indian reunification") under a secular government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Katju2020-0)[\[451\]](#refnote-Katju2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-468-0)[\[452\]](#refnote-468)
- According to lawyer and human rights activist [K. Balagopal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._Balagopal "K. Balagopal"), Kashmiris have a distinct sense of identity and this identity is certainly not irreligious, as Islam is very much a part of the identity that Kashmiris feel strongly for. He opined that if only non-religious identities deserve support, then no national self-determination movement can be supported, because there is no national identity – at least in the Third World – devoid of the religious dimension. Balagopal says that if India and Pakistan cannot guarantee existence and peaceful development of independent Kashmir then Kashmiris may well choose Pakistan because of religious affinity and social and economic links. But if both can guarantee existence and peaceful development then most Kashmiris would prefer independent Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-0)[\[453\]](#refnote-:4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-470-0)[\[454\]](#refnote-470)

### Water dispute

In 1948, [Eugene Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_R._Black_Sr. "Eugene R. Black Sr."), then executive director of the World Bank, offered his services to solve the tension over water control. In the early days of independence, the fact that India was able to shut off the Central Bari Doab Canals at the time of the sowing season, causing significant damage to Pakistan's crops. Nevertheless, military and political clashes over Kashmir in the early years of independence appear to have been more about ideology and sovereignty rather than over the sharing of water resources. However, the minister of Pakistan has stated the opposite.[&#8202;](#ref-back-471-0)[\[455\]](#refnote-471)

The [Indus Waters Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_Waters_Treaty "Indus Waters Treaty") was signed by both countries in September 1960, giving exclusive rights over the three western rivers of the Indus river system (Jhelum, Chenab and Indus) to Pakistan, and over the three eastern rivers (Sutlej, Ravi and Beas) to India, as long as this does not reduce or delay the supply to Pakistan. India therefore maintains that they are not willing to break the established regulations and they see no more problems with this issue.

## Efforts to end the dispute

There has been little meaningful dialogue to end the long-standing conflict. As of 2024, India holds the territorially advantageous position. Proposed solutions include independence for Kashmir, formal partition between India and Pakistan, and greater autonomy for the states of [Azad Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Kashmir "Azad Kashmir") and [Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(union_territory) "Jammu and Kashmir (union territory)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kurr_2015_p.-0)[\[456\]](#refnote-Kurr_2015_p.)

### Settlement formulas

#### Rajaji-Abdullah formula

The Rajaji-Abdullah formula, named after [C. Rajagopalachari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._Rajagopalachari "C. Rajagopalachari") and [Sheikh Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Abdullah "Sheikh Abdullah"), also simply called the Rajaji formula, was in Abdullah's words "an honourable solution which would not give a sense of victory either to India or Pakistan and at the same time would ensure a place of honour to the people of Kashmir".[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEGuha,_Opening_a_Window_in_Kashmir200487-0)[\[457\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEGuha,_Opening_a_Window_in_Kashmir200487) The discussions in 1964 covered numerous options, a plebiscite, status quo, further division etc., however seemed to point towards a [condominium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condominium_(international_law) "Condominium (international law)"), a shared government, a [confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederation "Confederation") and a [United Nations trust territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_trust_territories "United Nations trust territories").[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEGuha,_Opening_a_Window_in_Kashmir200487-1)[\[457\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEGuha,_Opening_a_Window_in_Kashmir200487)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:15-0)[\[458\]](#refnote-:15) Abdullah would act as the mediator between India and Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:15-1)[\[458\]](#refnote-:15) While the exact nature of the proposed settlement was never made public, Ayub Khan would go on to write in his autobiography that the proposal was "absurd".[&#8202;](#ref-back-475-0)[\[459\]](#refnote-475)

#### Chenab formula

In 2005, General Musharraf, as well as other Pakistani leaders, sought to resolve the Kashmir issue through the Chenab formula road map. Borrowing a term used by Owen Dixon, Musharraf's Chenab Formula assigns Ladakh to India, Gilgit-Baltistan (G-B) to Pakistan, proposes a plebiscite in the Kashmir Valley and splits Jammu into two-halves.[&#8202;](#ref-back-476-0)[\[460\]](#refnote-476) On 5 December 2006, Pakistani President [Pervez Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf "Pervez Musharraf") told an Indian TV channel that Pakistan would give up its claim on Kashmir if India accepted some of his peace proposals, including a phased withdrawal of troops, self-governance for locals, no changes in the borders of Kashmir, and a joint supervision mechanism involving India, Pakistan, and Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-477-0)[\[461\]](#refnote-477) Musharraf stated that he was ready to give up the United Nations' resolutions regarding Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-478-0)[\[462\]](#refnote-478)

Later, the government of Pakistan said that this was Musharraf's personal opinion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-479-0)[\[463\]](#refnote-479) However Satinder Lambah, India's special envoy to Pakistan, says that while talks reduced due to the Mumbai attacks, the formula was not disowned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-480-0)[\[464\]](#refnote-480)

### Contemporary views on UN resolutions

Many neutral parties to the dispute have noted that the UN resolution on Kashmir is no longer relevant.[&#8202;](#ref-back-481-0)[\[465\]](#refnote-481) The European Union holds the view that the plebiscite is not in Kashmiris' interest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-482-0)[\[466\]](#refnote-482) The report notes that the UN conditions for such a plebiscite have not been, and can no longer be, met by Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-483-0)[\[467\]](#refnote-483) The [Hurriyat Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurriyat_Conference "Hurriyat Conference") observed in 2003 that a "plebiscite \[&#8202;](#ref-back-484-0)[is\] no longer an option".[\[468\]](#refnote-484)

A 2002 [Market and Opinion Research International (MORI)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos_MORI "Ipsos MORI") survey, on the basis of 850 interviews, found that within Indian-administered Kashmir, 61% of respondents said they felt they would be better off as Indian citizens, with 33% saying that they did not know, and the remaining 6% favouring Pakistani citizenship. However, this support for India was mainly in the Ladakh and Jammu regions, not the Kashmir Valley, where only 9% of the respondents said that they would be better off with India.[&#8202;](#ref-back-485-0)[\[469\]](#refnote-485)\[ _[neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Neutral_point_of_view "Wikipedia:Neutral point of view") is [disputed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk:Kashmir_conflict# "Talk:Kashmir conflict")_\]

According to a 2007 poll conducted by the [Centre for the Study of Developing Societies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_the_Study_of_Developing_Societies "Centre for the Study of Developing Societies") in New Delhi involving around 400 people, 87% of respondents in the Kashmir Valley prefer independence over union with India or Pakistan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-486-0)[\[470\]](#refnote-486) A survey by [Chatham House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatham_House "Chatham House"), on the basis of 3,774 face-to face interviews in Indian administered [Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(state) "Jammu and Kashmir (state)") and Pakistan administered [Azad Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Kashmir "Azad Kashmir"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-487-0)[\[note 17\]](#refnote-487) found that support for independence stood at 43% and 44% respectively.[&#8202;](#ref-back-488-0)[\[471\]](#refnote-488)

## Pakistan's relation with militants

In a 2001 commentary entitled _Pakistan's Role in the Kashmir Insurgency_ in _[Jane's Intelligence Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane's_Intelligence_Review "Jane's Intelligence Review")_, the author noted that "the nature of the Kashmir conflict has been transformed from what was originally a secular, locally based struggle (conducted via the [Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_Kashmir_Liberation_Front "Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front")– JKLF) to one that is now largely carried out by foreign militants and rationalized in pan-Islamic religious terms." The majority of militant organisations are composed of foreign mercenaries, mostly from the [Pakistani Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab,_Pakistan "Punjab, Pakistan").[&#8202;](#ref-back-489-0)[\[472\]](#refnote-489)

In 2010, with the support of its intelligence agencies, Pakistan again 'boosted' Kashmir militants, and recruitment of [mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mujahideen "Mujahideen") in the Pakistani state of Punjab has increased.[&#8202;](#ref-back-490-0)[\[473\]](#refnote-490)

[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc05142010-0)[\[474\]](#refnote-bbc05142010) In 2011, the [FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI "FBI") revealed that Pakistan's spy agency [ISI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Services_Intelligence "Inter-Services Intelligence") paid millions of dollars into a United States–based non-governmental organisation to influence politicians and opinion-makers on the Kashmir issue and arrested [Syed Ghulam Nabi Fai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Ghulam_Nabi_Fai "Syed Ghulam Nabi Fai").[&#8202;](#ref-back-492-0)[\[475\]](#refnote-492)

Some political analysts say that the Pakistan state policy shift and mellowing of its aggressive stance may have to do with its total failure in the [Kargil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kargil_War "Kargil War") and the subsequent [9/11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks") attacks. These events put pressure on Pakistan to alter its position on terrorism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-493-0)[\[476\]](#refnote-493)

[Former President of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Pakistan "List of Presidents of Pakistan") and the ex-chief of the Pakistan military [Pervez Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf "Pervez Musharraf"), stated in an interview in London, that the Pakistani government indeed helped to form underground militant groups and "turned a blind eye" towards their existence because they wanted India to discuss Kashmir.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Speigel-0)[\[477\]](#refnote-Speigel)

According to former [Indian Prime-minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India "Prime Minister of India") [Manmohan Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manmohan_Singh "Manmohan Singh"), one of the main reasons behind the conflict was Pakistan's "terror-induced coercion". He further stated at a Joint Press Conference with United States President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") in New Delhi that India is not afraid of resolving all the issues with Pakistan including that of Kashmir " _but it is our request that you cannot simultaneously be talking and at the same time the terror machine is as active as ever before. Once Pakistan moves away from this terror-induced coercion, we will be very happy to engage productively with Pakistan to resolve all outstanding issues._"[&#8202;](#ref-back-495-0)[\[478\]](#refnote-495)
